# Pre-Landing PR Review

You are running the `/review` workflow. Analyze the current branch's diff against the base branch for structural issues that tests don't catch.

---

## Step 1: Check branch

1. Run `git branch --show-current` to get the current branch.
2. If on the base branch, output: **"Nothing to review â€” you're on the base branch or have no changes against it."** and stop.
3. Run `git fetch origin <base> --quiet && git diff origin/<base> --stat` to check if there's a diff. If no diff, output the same message and stop.

---

## Step 1.5: Scope Drift Detection

Before reviewing code quality, check: **did they build what was requested â€” nothing more, nothing less?**

1. Read `TODOS.md` (if it exists). Read PR description (`gh pr view --json body --jq .body 2>/dev/null || true`).
   Read commit messages (`git log origin/<base>..HEAD --oneline`).
   **If no PR exists:** rely on commit messages and TODOS.md for stated intent â€” this is the common case since /review runs before /ship creates the PR.
2. Identify the **stated intent** â€” what was this branch supposed to accomplish?
3. Run `git diff origin/<base>...HEAD --stat` and compare the files changed against the stated intent.

4. Evaluate with skepticism (incorporating plan completion results if available from an earlier step or adjacent section):

   **SCOPE CREEP detection:**
   - Files changed that are unrelated to the stated intent
   - New features or refactors not mentioned in the plan
   - "While I was in there..." changes that expand blast radius

   **MISSING REQUIREMENTS detection:**
   - Requirements from TODOS.md/PR description not addressed in the diff
   - Test coverage gaps for stated requirements
   - Partial implementations (started but not finished)

5. Output (before the main review begins):
   \`\`\`
   Scope Check: [CLEAN / DRIFT DETECTED / REQUIREMENTS MISSING]
   Intent: <1-line summary of what was requested>
   Delivered: <1-line summary of what the diff actually does>
   [If drift: list each out-of-scope change]
   [If missing: list each unaddressed requirement]
   \`\`\`

6. This is **INFORMATIONAL** â€” does not block the review. Proceed to the next step.

---

### Plan File Discovery

1. **Conversation context (primary):** Check if there is an active plan file in this conversation. The host agent's system messages include plan file paths when in plan mode. If found, use it directly â€” this is the most reliable signal.

2. **Content-based search (fallback):** If no plan file is referenced in conversation context, search by content:

```bash
setopt +o nomatch 2>/dev/null || true  # zsh compat
BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current 2>/dev/null | tr '/' '-')
REPO=$(basename "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null)")
# Compute project slug for ~/.gstack/projects/ lookup
_PLAN_SLUG=$(git remote get-url origin 2>/dev/null | sed 's|.*[:/]\([^/]*/[^/]*\)\.git$|\1|;s|.*[:/]\([^/]*/[^/]*\)$|\1|' | tr '/' '-' | tr -cd 'a-zA-Z0-9._-') || true
_PLAN_SLUG="${_PLAN_SLUG:-$(basename "$PWD" | tr -cd 'a-zA-Z0-9._-')}"
# Search common plan file locations (project designs first, then personal/local)
for PLAN_DIR in "$HOME/.gstack/projects/$_PLAN_SLUG" "$HOME/.claude/plans" "$HOME/.codex/plans" ".gstack/plans"; do
  [ -d "$PLAN_DIR" ] || continue
  PLAN=$(ls -t "$PLAN_DIR"/*.md 2>/dev/null | xargs grep -l "$BRANCH" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
  [ -z "$PLAN" ] && PLAN=$(ls -t "$PLAN_DIR"/*.md 2>/dev/null | xargs grep -l "$REPO" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
  [ -z "$PLAN" ] && PLAN=$(find "$PLAN_DIR" -name '*.md' -mmin -1440 -maxdepth 1 2>/dev/null | xargs ls -t 2>/dev/null | head -1)
  [ -n "$PLAN" ] && break
done
[ -n "$PLAN" ] && echo "PLAN_FILE: $PLAN" || echo "NO_PLAN_FILE"
```

3. **Validation:** If a plan file was found via content-based search (not conversation context), read the first 20 lines and verify it is relevant to the current branch's work. If it appears to be from a different project or feature, treat as "no plan file found."

**Error handling:**
- No plan file found â†’ skip with "No plan file detected â€” skipping."
- Plan file found but unreadable (permissions, encoding) â†’ skip with "Plan file found but unreadable â€” skipping."

### Actionable Item Extraction

Read the plan file. Extract every actionable item â€” anything that describes work to be done. Look for:

- **Checkbox items:** `- [ ] ...` or `- [x] ...`
- **Numbered steps** under implementation headings: "1. Create ...", "2. Add ...", "3. Modify ..."
- **Imperative statements:** "Add X to Y", "Create a Z service", "Modify the W controller"
- **File-level specifications:** "New file: path/to/file.ts", "Modify path/to/existing.rb"
- **Test requirements:** "Test that X", "Add test for Y", "Verify Z"
- **Data model changes:** "Add column X to table Y", "Create migration for Z"

**Ignore:**
- Context/Background sections (`## Context`, `## Background`, `## Problem`)
- Questions and open items (marked with ?, "TBD", "TODO: decide")
- Review report sections (`## GSTACK REVIEW REPORT`)
- Explicitly deferred items ("Future:", "Out of scope:", "NOT in scope:", "P2:", "P3:", "P4:")
- CEO Review Decisions sections (these record choices, not work items)

**Cap:** Extract at most 50 items. If the plan has more, note: "Showing top 50 of N plan items â€” full list in plan file."

**No items found:** If the plan contains no extractable actionable items, skip with: "Plan file contains no actionable items â€” skipping completion audit."

For each item, note:
- The item text (verbatim or concise summary)
- Its category: CODE | TEST | MIGRATION | CONFIG | DOCS

### Cross-Reference Against Diff

Run `git diff origin/<base>...HEAD` and `git log origin/<base>..HEAD --oneline` to understand what was implemented.

For each extracted plan item, check the diff and classify:

- **DONE** â€” Clear evidence in the diff that this item was implemented. Cite the specific file(s) changed.
- **PARTIAL** â€” Some work toward this item exists in the diff but it's incomplete (e.g., model created but controller missing, function exists but edge cases not handled).
- **NOT DONE** â€” No evidence in the diff that this item was addressed.
- **CHANGED** â€” The item was implemented using a different approach than the plan described, but the same goal is achieved. Note the difference.

**Be conservative with DONE** â€” require clear evidence in the diff. A file being touched is not enough; the specific functionality described must be present.
**Be generous with CHANGED** â€” if the goal is met by different means, that counts as addressed.

### Output Format

```
PLAN COMPLETION AUDIT
â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•
Plan: {plan file path}

## Implementation Items
  [DONE]      Create UserService â€” src/services/user_service.rb (+142 lines)
  [PARTIAL]   Add validation â€” model validates but missing controller checks
  [NOT DONE]  Add caching layer â€” no cache-related changes in diff
  [CHANGED]   "Redis queue" â†’ implemented with Sidekiq instead

## Test Items
  [DONE]      Unit tests for UserService â€” test/services/user_service_test.rb
  [NOT DONE]  E2E test for signup flow

## Migration Items
  [DONE]      Create users table â€” db/migrate/20240315_create_users.rb

â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
COMPLETION: 4/7 DONE, 1 PARTIAL, 1 NOT DONE, 1 CHANGED
â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
```

### Fallback Intent Sources (when no plan file found)

When no plan file is detected, use these secondary intent sources:

1. **Commit messages:** Run `git log origin/<base>..HEAD --oneline`. Use judgment to extract real intent:
   - Commits with actionable verbs ("add", "implement", "fix", "create", "remove", "update") are intent signals
   - Skip noise: "WIP", "tmp", "squash", "merge", "chore", "typo", "fixup"
   - Extract the intent behind the commit, not the literal message
2. **TODOS.md:** If it exists, check for items related to this branch or recent dates
3. **PR description:** Run `gh pr view --json body -q .body 2>/dev/null` for intent context

**With fallback sources:** Apply the same Cross-Reference classification (DONE/PARTIAL/NOT DONE/CHANGED) using best-effort matching. Note that fallback-sourced items are lower confidence than plan-file items.

### Investigation Depth

For each PARTIAL or NOT DONE item, investigate WHY:

1. Check `git log origin/<base>..HEAD --oneline` for commits that suggest the work was started, attempted, or reverted
2. Read the relevant code to understand what was built instead
3. Determine the likely reason from this list:
   - **Scope cut** â€” evidence of intentional removal (revert commit, removed TODO)
   - **Context exhaustion** â€” work started but stopped mid-way (partial implementation, no follow-up commits)
   - **Misunderstood requirement** â€” something was built but it doesn't match what the plan described
   - **Blocked by dependency** â€” plan item depends on something that isn't available
   - **Genuinely forgotten** â€” no evidence of any attempt

Output for each discrepancy:
```
DISCREPANCY: {PARTIAL|NOT_DONE} | {plan item} | {what was actually delivered}
INVESTIGATION: {likely reason with evidence from git log / code}
IMPACT: {HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW} â€” {what breaks or degrades if this stays undelivered}

[35m[3mexec[0m[0m
[1m"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '$c=Get-Content '"'C:\\Users\\zwl62\\.codex\\skills\\gstack-review\\SKILL.md'; "'$c[300..699]'[0m in D:\omega\automath\papers\publication\2026_upper_fibers_witness_covers_fibonacci_apparition_fq
[32m succeeded in 198ms:[0m

If `RECENT_PATTERN` is shown, look at the skill sequence. If a pattern repeats
(e.g., review,ship,review), suggest: "Based on your recent pattern, you probably
want /[next skill]."

**Welcome back message:** If any of LAST_SESSION, LATEST_CHECKPOINT, or RECENT ARTIFACTS
are shown, synthesize a one-paragraph welcome briefing before proceeding:
"Welcome back to {branch}. Last session: /{skill} ({outcome}). [Checkpoint summary if
available]. [Health score if available]." Keep it to 2-3 sentences.

## AskUserQuestion Format

**ALWAYS follow this structure for every AskUserQuestion call:**
1. **Re-ground:** State the project, the current branch (use the `_BRANCH` value printed by the preamble â€” NOT any branch from conversation history or gitStatus), and the current plan/task. (1-2 sentences)
2. **Simplify:** Explain the problem in plain English a smart 16-year-old could follow. No raw function names, no internal jargon, no implementation details. Use concrete examples and analogies. Say what it DOES, not what it's called.
3. **Recommend:** `RECOMMENDATION: Choose [X] because [one-line reason]` â€” always prefer the complete option over shortcuts (see Completeness Principle). Include `Completeness: X/10` for each option. Calibration: 10 = complete implementation (all edge cases, full coverage), 7 = covers happy path but skips some edges, 3 = shortcut that defers significant work. If both options are 8+, pick the higher; if one is â‰¤5, flag it.
4. **Options:** Lettered options: `A) ... B) ... C) ...` â€” when an option involves effort, show both scales: `(human: ~X / CC: ~Y)`

Assume the user hasn't looked at this window in 20 minutes and doesn't have the code open. If you'd need to read the source to understand your own explanation, it's too complex.

Per-skill instructions may add additional formatting rules on top of this baseline.

## Completeness Principle â€” Boil the Lake

AI makes completeness near-free. Always recommend the complete option over shortcuts â€” the delta is minutes with CC+gstack. A "lake" (100% coverage, all edge cases) is boilable; an "ocean" (full rewrite, multi-quarter migration) is not. Boil lakes, flag oceans.

**Effort reference** â€” always show both scales:

| Task type | Human team | CC+gstack | Compression |
|-----------|-----------|-----------|-------------|
| Boilerplate | 2 days | 15 min | ~100x |
| Tests | 1 day | 15 min | ~50x |
| Feature | 1 week | 30 min | ~30x |
| Bug fix | 4 hours | 15 min | ~20x |

Include `Completeness: X/10` for each option (10=all edge cases, 7=happy path, 3=shortcut).

## Repo Ownership â€” See Something, Say Something

`REPO_MODE` controls how to handle issues outside your branch:
- **`solo`** â€” You own everything. Investigate and offer to fix proactively.
- **`collaborative`** / **`unknown`** â€” Flag via AskUserQuestion, don't fix (may be someone else's).

Always flag anything that looks wrong â€” one sentence, what you noticed and its impact.

## Search Before Building

Before building anything unfamiliar, **search first.** See `$GSTACK_ROOT/ETHOS.md`.
- **Layer 1** (tried and true) â€” don't reinvent. **Layer 2** (new and popular) â€” scrutinize. **Layer 3** (first principles) â€” prize above all.

**Eureka:** When first-principles reasoning contradicts conventional wisdom, name it and log:
```bash
jq -n --arg ts "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" --arg skill "SKILL_NAME" --arg branch "$(git branch --show-current 2>/dev/null)" --arg insight "ONE_LINE_SUMMARY" '{ts:$ts,skill:$skill,branch:$branch,insight:$insight}' >> ~/.gstack/analytics/eureka.jsonl 2>/dev/null || true
```

## Completion Status Protocol

When completing a skill workflow, report status using one of:
- **DONE** â€” All steps completed successfully. Evidence provided for each claim.
- **DONE_WITH_CONCERNS** â€” Completed, but with issues the user should know about. List each concern.
- **BLOCKED** â€” Cannot proceed. State what is blocking and what was tried.
- **NEEDS_CONTEXT** â€” Missing information required to continue. State exactly what you need.

### Escalation

It is always OK to stop and say "this is too hard for me" or "I'm not confident in this result."

Bad work is worse than no work. You will not be penalized for escalating.
- If you have attempted a task 3 times without success, STOP and escalate.
- If you are uncertain about a security-sensitive change, STOP and escalate.
- If the scope of work exceeds what you can verify, STOP and escalate.

Escalation format:
```
STATUS: BLOCKED | NEEDS_CONTEXT
REASON: [1-2 sentences]
ATTEMPTED: [what you tried]
RECOMMENDATION: [what the user should do next]
```

## Operational Self-Improvement

Before completing, reflect on this session:
- Did any commands fail unexpectedly?
- Did you take a wrong approach and have to backtrack?
- Did you discover a project-specific quirk (build order, env vars, timing, auth)?
- Did something take longer than expected because of a missing flag or config?

If yes, log an operational learning for future sessions:

```bash
$GSTACK_BIN/gstack-learnings-log '{"skill":"SKILL_NAME","type":"operational","key":"SHORT_KEY","insight":"DESCRIPTION","confidence":N,"source":"observed"}'
```

Replace SKILL_NAME with the current skill name. Only log genuine operational discoveries.
Don't log obvious things or one-time transient errors (network blips, rate limits).
A good test: would knowing this save 5+ minutes in a future session? If yes, log it.

## Telemetry (run last)

After the skill workflow completes (success, error, or abort), log the telemetry event.
Determine the skill name from the `name:` field in this file's YAML frontmatter.
Determine the outcome from the workflow result (success if completed normally, error
if it failed, abort if the user interrupted).

**PLAN MODE EXCEPTION â€” ALWAYS RUN:** This command writes telemetry to
`~/.gstack/analytics/` (user config directory, not project files). The skill
preamble already writes to the same directory â€” this is the same pattern.
Skipping this command loses session duration and outcome data.

Run this bash:

```bash
_TEL_END=$(date +%s)
_TEL_DUR=$(( _TEL_END - _TEL_START ))
rm -f ~/.gstack/analytics/.pending-"$_SESSION_ID" 2>/dev/null || true
# Session timeline: record skill completion (local-only, never sent anywhere)
$GSTACK_ROOT/bin/gstack-timeline-log '{"skill":"SKILL_NAME","event":"completed","branch":"'$(git branch --show-current 2>/dev/null || echo unknown)'","outcome":"OUTCOME","duration_s":"'"$_TEL_DUR"'","session":"'"$_SESSION_ID"'"}' 2>/dev/null || true
# Local analytics (gated on telemetry setting)
if [ "$_TEL" != "off" ]; then
echo '{"skill":"SKILL_NAME","duration_s":"'"$_TEL_DUR"'","outcome":"OUTCOME","browse":"USED_BROWSE","session":"'"$_SESSION_ID"'","ts":"'$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)'"}' >> ~/.gstack/analytics/skill-usage.jsonl 2>/dev/null || true
fi
# Remote telemetry (opt-in, requires binary)
if [ "$_TEL" != "off" ] && [ -x $GSTACK_ROOT/bin/gstack-telemetry-log ]; then
  $GSTACK_ROOT/bin/gstack-telemetry-log \
    --skill "SKILL_NAME" --duration "$_TEL_DUR" --outcome "OUTCOME" \
    --used-browse "USED_BROWSE" --session-id "$_SESSION_ID" 2>/dev/null &
fi
```

Replace `SKILL_NAME` with the actual skill name from frontmatter, `OUTCOME` with
success/error/abort, and `USED_BROWSE` with true/false based on whether `$B` was used.
If you cannot determine the outcome, use "unknown". The local JSONL always logs. The
remote binary only runs if telemetry is not off and the binary exists.

## Plan Mode Safe Operations

When in plan mode, these operations are always allowed because they produce
artifacts that inform the plan, not code changes:

- `$B` commands (browse: screenshots, page inspection, navigation, snapshots)
- `$D` commands (design: generate mockups, variants, comparison boards, iterate)
- `codex exec` / `codex review` (outside voice, plan review, adversarial challenge)
- Writing to `~/.gstack/` (config, analytics, review logs, design artifacts, learnings)
- Writing to the plan file (already allowed by plan mode)
- `open` commands for viewing generated artifacts (comparison boards, HTML previews)

These are read-only in spirit â€” they inspect the live site, generate visual artifacts,
or get independent opinions. They do NOT modify project source files.

## Plan Status Footer

When you are in plan mode and about to call ExitPlanMode:

1. Check if the plan file already has a `## GSTACK REVIEW REPORT` section.
2. If it DOES â€” skip (a review skill already wrote a richer report).
3. If it does NOT â€” run this command:

\`\`\`bash
$GSTACK_ROOT/bin/gstack-review-read
\`\`\`

Then write a `## GSTACK REVIEW REPORT` section to the end of the plan file:

- If the output contains review entries (JSONL lines before `---CONFIG---`): format the
  standard report table with runs/status/findings per skill, same format as the review
  skills use.
- If the output is `NO_REVIEWS` or empty: write this placeholder table:

\`\`\`markdown
## GSTACK REVIEW REPORT

| Review | Trigger | Why | Runs | Status | Findings |
|--------|---------|-----|------|--------|----------|
| CEO Review | \`/plan-ceo-review\` | Scope & strategy | 0 | â€” | â€” |
| Codex Review | \`/codex review\` | Independent 2nd opinion | 0 | â€” | â€” |
| Eng Review | \`/plan-eng-review\` | Architecture & tests (required) | 0 | â€” | â€” |
| Design Review | \`/plan-design-review\` | UI/UX gaps | 0 | â€” | â€” |

**VERDICT:** NO REVIEWS YET â€” run \`/autoplan\` for full review pipeline, or individual reviews above.
\`\`\`

**PLAN MODE EXCEPTION â€” ALWAYS RUN:** This writes to the plan file, which is the one
file you are allowed to edit in plan mode. The plan file review report is part of the
plan's living status.

## Step 0: Detect platform and base branch

First, detect the git hosting platform from the remote URL:

```bash
git remote get-url origin 2>/dev/null
```

- If the URL contains "github.com" â†’ platform is **GitHub**
- If the URL contains "gitlab" â†’ platform is **GitLab**
- Otherwise, check CLI availability:
  - `gh auth status 2>/dev/null` succeeds â†’ platform is **GitHub** (covers GitHub Enterprise)
  - `glab auth status 2>/dev/null` succeeds â†’ platform is **GitLab** (covers self-hosted)
  - Neither â†’ **unknown** (use git-native commands only)

Determine which branch this PR/MR targets, or the repo's default branch if no
PR/MR exists. Use the result as "the base branch" in all subsequent steps.

**If GitHub:**
1. `gh pr view --json baseRefName -q .baseRefName` â€” if succeeds, use it
2. `gh repo view --json defaultBranchRef -q .defaultBranchRef.name` â€” if succeeds, use it

**If GitLab:**
1. `glab mr view -F json 2>/dev/null` and extract the `target_branch` field â€” if succeeds, use it
2. `glab repo view -F json 2>/dev/null` and extract the `default_branch` field â€” if succeeds, use it

**Git-native fallback (if unknown platform, or CLI commands fail):**
1. `git symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD 2>/dev/null | sed 's|refs/remotes/origin/||'`
2. If that fails: `git rev-parse --verify origin/main 2>/dev/null` â†’ use `main`
3. If that fails: `git rev-parse --verify origin/master 2>/dev/null` â†’ use `master`

If all fail, fall back to `main`.

Print the detected base branch name. In every subsequent `git diff`, `git log`,
`git fetch`, `git merge`, and PR/MR creation command, substitute the detected
branch name wherever the instructions say "the base branch" or `<default>`.

---

# Pre-Landing PR Review

You are running the `/review` workflow. Analyze the current branch's diff against the base branch for structural issues that tests don't catch.

---

## Step 1: Check branch

1. Run `git branch --show-current` to get the current branch.
2. If on the base branch, output: **"Nothing to review â€” you're on the base branch or have no changes against it."** and stop.
3. Run `git fetch origin <base> --quiet && git diff origin/<base> --stat` to check if there's a diff. If no diff, output the same message and stop.

---

## Step 1.5: Scope Drift Detection

Before reviewing code quality, check: **did they build what was requested â€” nothing more, nothing less?**

1. Read `TODOS.md` (if it exists). Read PR description (`gh pr view --json body --jq .body 2>/dev/null || true`).
   Read commit messages (`git log origin/<base>..HEAD --oneline`).
   **If no PR exists:** rely on commit messages and TODOS.md for stated intent â€” this is the common case since /review runs before /ship creates the PR.
2. Identify the **stated intent** â€” what was this branch supposed to accomplish?
3. Run `git diff origin/<base>...HEAD --stat` and compare the files changed against the stated intent.

4. Evaluate with skepticism (incorporating plan completion results if available from an earlier step or adjacent section):

   **SCOPE CREEP detection:**
   - Files changed that are unrelated to the stated intent
   - New features or refactors not mentioned in the plan
   - "While I was in there..." changes that expand blast radius

   **MISSING REQUIREMENTS detection:**
   - Requirements from TODOS.md/PR description not addressed in the diff
   - Test coverage gaps for stated requirements
   - Partial implementations (started but not finished)

5. Output (before the main review begins):
   \`\`\`
   Scope Check: [CLEAN / DRIFT DETECTED / REQUIREMENTS MISSING]
   Intent: <1-line summary of what was requested>
   Delivered: <1-line summary of what the diff actually does>
   [If drift: list each out-of-scope change]
   [If missing: list each unaddressed requirement]
   \`\`\`

6. This is **INFORMATIONAL** â€” does not block the review. Proceed to the next step.

---

### Plan File Discovery

1. **Conversation context (primary):** Check if there is an active plan file in this conversation. The host agent's system messages include plan file paths when in plan mode. If found, use it directly â€” this is the most reliable signal.

2. **Content-based search (fallback):** If no plan file is referenced in conversation context, search by content:

```bash
setopt +o nomatch 2>/dev/null || true  # zsh compat
BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current 2>/dev/null | tr '/' '-')
REPO=$(basename "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null)")
# Compute project slug for ~/.gstack/projects/ lookup
_PLAN_SLUG=$(git remote get-url origin 2>/dev/null | sed 's|.*[:/]\([^/]*/[^/]*\)\.git$|\1|;s|.*[:/]\([^/]*/[^/]*\)$|\1|' | tr '/' '-' | tr -cd 'a-zA-Z0-9._-') || true
_PLAN_SLUG="${_PLAN_SLUG:-$(basename "$PWD" | tr -cd 'a-zA-Z0-9._-')}"
# Search common plan file locations (project designs first, then personal/local)
for PLAN_DIR in "$HOME/.gstack/projects/$_PLAN_SLUG" "$HOME/.claude/plans" "$HOME/.codex/plans" ".gstack/plans"; do
  [ -d "$PLAN_DIR" ] || continue
  PLAN=$(ls -t "$PLAN_DIR"/*.md 2>/dev/null | xargs grep -l "$BRANCH" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
  [ -z "$PLAN" ] && PLAN=$(ls -t "$PLAN_DIR"/*.md 2>/dev/null | xargs grep -l "$REPO" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
  [ -z "$PLAN" ] && PLAN=$(find "$PLAN_DIR" -name '*.md' -mmin -1440 -maxdepth 1 2>/dev/null | xargs ls -t 2>/dev/null | head -1)
  [ -n "$PLAN" ] && break
done
[ -n "$PLAN" ] && echo "PLAN_FILE: $PLAN" || echo "NO_PLAN_FILE"
```

3. **Validation:** If a plan file was found via content-based search (not conversation context), read the first 20 lines and verify it is relevant to the current branch's work. If it appears to be from a different project or feature, treat as "no plan file found."

**Error handling:**
- No plan file found â†’ skip with "No plan file detected â€” skipping."
- Plan file found but unreadable (permissions, encoding) â†’ skip with "Plan file found but unreadable â€” skipping."

### Actionable Item Extraction

Read the plan file. Extract every actionable item â€” anything that describes work to be done. Look for:

- **Checkbox items:** `- [ ] ...` or `- [x] ...`
- **Numbered steps** under implementation headings: "1. Create ...", "2. Add ...", "3. Modify ..."
- **Imperative statements:** "Add X to Y", "Create a Z service", "Modify the W controller"
- **File-level specifications:** "New file: path/to/file.ts", "Modify path/to/existing.rb"
- **Test requirements:** "Test that X", "Add test for Y", "Verify Z"
- **Data model changes:** "Add column X to table Y", "Create migration for Z"

**Ignore:**
- Context/Background sections (`## Context`, `## Background`, `## Problem`)
- Questions and open items (marked with ?, "TBD", "TODO: decide")
- Review report sections (`## GSTACK REVIEW REPORT`)
- Explicitly deferred items ("Future:", "Out of scope:", "NOT in scope:", "P2:", "P3:", "P4:")
- CEO Review Decisions sections (these record choices, not work items)

**Cap:** Extract at most 50 items. If the plan has more, note: "Showing top 50 of N plan items â€” full list in plan file."

**No items found:** If the plan contains no extractable actionable items, skip with: "Plan file contains no actionable items â€” skipping completion audit."

For each item, note:
- The item text (verbatim or concise summary)
- Its category: CODE | TEST | MIGRATION | CONFIG | DOCS

### Cross-Reference Against Diff

Run `git diff origin/<base>...HEAD` and `git log origin/<base>..HEAD --oneline` to understand what was implemented.

For each extracted plan item, check the diff and classify:

- **DONE** â€” Clear evidence in the diff that this item was implemented. Cite the specific file(s) changed.
- **PARTIAL** â€” Some work toward this item exists in the diff but it's incomplete (e.g., model created but controller missing, function exists but edge cases not handled).
- **NOT DONE** â€” No evidence in the diff that this item was addressed.
- **CHANGED** â€” The item was implemented using a different approach than the plan described, but the same goal is achieved. Note the difference.

**Be conservative with DONE** â€” require clear evidence in the diff. A file being touched is not enough; the specific functionality described must be present.
**Be generous with CHANGED** â€” if the goal is met by different means, that counts as addressed.

### Output Format

```
PLAN COMPLETION AUDIT
â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•â•

### Fallback Intent Sources (when no plan file found)

When no plan file is detected, use these secondary intent sources:

1. **Commit messages:** Run `git log origin/<base>..HEAD --oneline`. Use judgment to extract real intent:
   - Commits with actionable verbs ("add", "implement", "fix", "create", "remove", "update") are intent signals
   - Skip noise: "WIP", "tmp", "squash", "merge", "chore", "typo", "fixup"
   - Extract the intent behind the commit, not the literal message
2. **TODOS.md:** If it exists, check for items related to this branch or recent dates
3. **PR description:** Run `gh pr view --json body -q .body 2>/dev/null` for intent context

**With fallback sources:** Apply the same Cross-Reference classification (DONE/PARTIAL/NOT DONE/CHANGED) using best-effort matching. Note that fallback-sourced items are lower confidence than plan-file items.

### Investigation Depth

For each PARTIAL or NOT DONE item, investigate WHY:

1. Check `git log origin/<base>..HEAD --oneline` for commits that suggest the work was started, attempted, or reverted
2. Read the relevant code to understand what was built instead
3. Determine the likely reason from this list:
   - **Scope cut** â€” evidence of intentional removal (revert commit, removed TODO)
   - **Context exhaustion** â€” work started but stopped mid-way (partial implementation, no follow-up commits)
   - **Misunderstood requirement** â€” something was built but it doesn't match what the plan described
   - **Blocked by dependency** â€” plan item depends on something that isn't available
   - **Genuinely forgotten** â€” no evidence of any attempt

Output for each discrepancy:

IMPACT: {HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW} â€” {what breaks or degrades if this stays undelivered}

The live paper’s candidates for the headline are the exact support-spectrum theorem, rank-pure universality, and the fibotomic entropy bound; I’m checking which has the strongest mathematical content rather than following the introduction’s marketing.

\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}
%% ====================================================================

For an integer $q\ge 1$, the \emph{rank of apparition} (or \emph{order of
appearance}) $\alpha(q)$ is the least positive index $r$ such that $q\mid
F_r$, where $(F_r)_{r\ge 0}$ denotes the Fibonacci sequence with $F_0=0$,
$F_1=1$; the existence of this least index for every $q$ is recorded in
Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-rank}. We use the harmless conventions $\alpha(1)=1$ and
$D_1=\operatorname{Div}(F_1)=\{1\}$ in divisor-lattice formulas. For each
$n\ge 2$, write
\[
D_n:=\operatorname{Div}(F_n),
\qquad
B_n:=\{q\in D_n:\ \alpha(q)=n\},
\qquad
A(n):=\#B_n.
\]
Let $\mathcal P(n)$ be the set of prime divisors of $n$, and let
$\mathcal M_n$ denote the set of minimal elements of $B_n$ under divisibility.
Thus $B_n$ consists of those moduli that first divide a Fibonacci number at
index~$n$. Since $F_2=1$, the boundary layer is empty: throughout the paper
we use the convention $B_2=\mathcal M_2=\varnothing$ and $A(2)=0$.
Throughout, $\log$ is the natural logarithm, $\omega(t)$ is the number of
distinct prime divisors of $t$, and $\Omega(t)$ counts prime divisors with
multiplicity. We write $A_{\rm pr}(n)$ for the number of primes of exact rank
$n$, $\mathsf{Part}(X)$ for the set of set partitions of a finite set $X$,
and $\mathsf B_k$ for the $k$th Bell number. A hypergraph on one vertex is
regarded as connected. These conventions also apply when the notation first
appears in a later section.
The counting function $A(n)$ is subsumed by Stroi\'nski's general
$\alpha$-contraction formalism \cite{Stroinski2016AlphaContraction}; see also
the classical M\"obius inversions discussed in
\cite{Halton1966DivisibilityFibonacci,FultonMorris1969ArithmeticalFunctions}.
At the prime level, Kiss proved an asymptotic for the accumulated logarithmic
mass of primitive parts and deduced the cumulative $x^2/\log x$ upper-bound
scale for distinct primitive primes \cite{Kiss1988PrimitiveDivisors};
Stroi\'nski's Theorem~7 gives the corresponding explicit limsup constant.
These results count fibers or exact-rank primes cumulatively; they do not study
the divisibility order on a fixed fiber or its minimal elements. Our scope is
the latter: we study $B_n$ as an ordered subset of $(D_n,\mid)$, asking how it
sits inside $D_n$, how it is generated, and what combinatorial structure it
carries.

Three classical facts underlie the analysis. First, $q\mid F_m$ if and only if
$\alpha(q)\mid m$
\cite{Wall1960FibonacciModm,Vinson1963RankApparition}. Second, the Fibonacci
sequence satisfies strong divisibility: $\gcd(F_a,F_b)=F_{\gcd(a,b)}$.
Third, ranks of apparition respect least common multiples:
$\alpha(\lcmop(u,v))=\lcmop(\alpha(u),\alpha(v))$; Renault proves the
corresponding identity in the more general $(a,b)$-Fibonacci setting
\cite{Renault2013PeriodRankOrder}. These properties, combined with the
primitive divisor theorem for Fibonacci numbers
\cite{Carmichael1913PrimitiveDivisors,BiluHanrotVoutier2001PrimitiveDivisorsLucas},
are the sole inputs for the main results. The Bilu--Hanrot--Voutier theorem is
used here only to supply an exact-rank prime at every required nonexceptional
index; it does not control how many exact-rank primes an index has.

This point of view complements several nearby strands in the literature.
Luca and Pomerance \cite{LucaPomerance2014LocalBehavior} study the local
behavior of $\alpha$ under additive shifts; Trojovsk\'a
\cite{Trojovska2020PeriodicPoints} and FitzGibbons--Javaheri--Miller--Verga
\cite{FitzGibbonsJavaheriMillerVerga2025Dynamics} study periodic points and
iteration dynamics of $\alpha$; Byer--Dvorachek--Eckard--Harrington--Wise--Wong
\cite{CameronCountsLundMillerPiechnikWong2022Fibotomic} study fibotomic
polynomials and related algebraic factorizations; and Sanna
\cite{Sanna2016LucasSequences} and Marques \cite{Marques2012OrderAppearance}
study the growth and fixed-point behavior of the order-of-appearance map. The
present paper instead fixes a single fiber $B_n$ and studies its divisibility
geometry inside $D_n$. The upper-fiber identity, the coordinatewise minimal-lcm
criterion, the prime--ladder alternative, the private-coordinate injection, and
the coprimality of disjoint coordinate blocks are recorded below as lemmas:
they are formal consequences of the cited classical identities and are not
claimed as independent arithmetic advances. The contributions developed from
these facts are the witness-cover formulation, the exhaustive support-three
classification with its arithmetic realization test, the passage of connected
block factorization to minimal elements, and the multiplicity-free
support-entropy coefficient $\log 2/4$. A further contribution is the
support-preserving rank-pure
embedding of arbitrary minimal set covers, which transfers the classical theta
asymptotic and a discrete central local limit to an explicit Fibonacci sector.
These lower results isolate, rather than resolve, the arithmetic multiplicity
$R(n)$. Accordingly, we make no unconditional claim of a sharp asymptotic for
$\#\mathcal M_n$ or of the negligibility of its disconnected part. We do,
however, isolate the mean interface further: support-window aggregation costs
only $O(x\log\log x)$ on average, so only a visible single-rank maximum remains.
A fibotomic size estimate and the congruence restrictions on exact-rank primes
then yield a uniform entropy inequality for each exact-rank class and a
pointwise bound of order $\phi_{\rm E}(d)/\log d$. This improves the available
single-rank input but does not settle the mean sparse-window hypothesis.
A conditional dominance theorem also identifies the stronger blockwise control
that would make the disconnected part exponentially negligible. The mean
sparse-window hypothesis is not presented as a prediction: Conjecture~5.1 of
Bugeaud--Luca--Mignotte--Siksek
\cite{BugeaudLucaMignotteSiksek2005FewPrimeDivisors} would imply its failure.

Call a prime power $\theta=p^e$ \emph{atomic for the index $d$} if
$\alpha(\theta)=d$ and $\alpha(p^{e-1})<d$. An \emph{$n$-witness cover} is a
pairwise coprime set of such atomic prime powers whose apparition indices
collectively span the prime factorization of $n$ in a minimal, irredundant
fashion; the full definition is Definition~\ref{def:witness-cover}.

\begin{previewtheorem}[Witness-cover theorem]
For every $n\ge2$, multiplication gives a bijection from $n$-witness
covers $\Theta$ to minimal generators:
\[
\mathcal M_n=
\left\{\prod_{\theta\in\Theta}\theta:\ \Theta\text{ is an $n$-witness cover}
\right\}.
\]
Consequently every $m\in\mathcal M_n$ satisfies
$\omega(m)\le\omega(n)$. This support budget is globally sharp in the precise
sense that no smaller bound depending only on $\omega(n)$ is valid; equality
is constructed on every odd layer $n\ge3$. The precise cover conditions are
given in Definition~\ref{def:witness-cover}, and the result is
Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-classification} together with
Corollaries~\ref{cor:support-bound},~\ref{cor:support-bound-sharp},
and the \suppref{Corollary ``Best possible support budget''}.
\end{previewtheorem}

The witness classification also controls the number of minimal generators,
not merely their form.

\begin{previewtheorem}[Support-entropy theorem]
For $n\ge3$, let $k=\omega(n)$ and let $R(n)$ be the largest atomic-family
multiplicity at one essential/full support pair. If $k\ge3$ and
$r=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$, then
\[
 (2^r-1)^{k-r}\le\#\mathcal M_n
 \le\sum_{s=1}^{k}\binom{k}{s}(2R(n))^s2^{s(k-s)}.
\]
The rank-pure sector gives the stronger connected lower bound
\[
 \log\#\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\ge
 \frac{\log2}{4}k^2+k\log2-\frac12\log k+O(1)
\]
uniformly as $k\to\infty$, with an explicit parity-dependent theta constant.
No exact quadratic coefficient is claimed for the full quantity
$\log\#\mathcal M_n$. The coefficient $\log2/4$ is exact only for the
unweighted minimal-cover count embedded in the fixed-choice rank-pure sector.
This assertion is not an optimality statement for all information obtainable
from fibotomic sizes and exact-rank congruences. These lower bounds do not give
an asymptotic formula for $\log\#\mathcal M_n$ unless
$R(n)=\exp(o(\omega(n)))$. Separate
conjectures about $R(n)$ imply conditional normal-order and mean equivalents.
These statements are
Theorems~\ref{thm:rank-pure-universality} and
\ref{thm:minimal-generator-growth}, together with their corollaries.
\end{previewtheorem}

The prime-power apparition formulas of Fulton--Morris
\cite{FultonMorris1969ArithmeticalFunctions} and Lengyel
\cite{Lengyel1995OrderFibonacciLucas} make the classification effective:
every atom is a prime atom or lies on a prime-power ladder changing one index
coordinate. The complete cases $\omega(n)\le3$, including the eight
support-three families, their finite slot test, and the induced description of
$B_n$, are proved in the \suppref{section ``Low-support classifications''}.
At the opposite endpoint, Theorem~\ref{thm:exact-support-spectra} classifies
every support size that occurs. The total spectrum is the full interval
$\{1,\ldots,\omega(n)\}$ except at even squarefree indices, where its top
value is absent. The connected spectrum is also an interval; its endpoint is
decided by squarefreeness and one oriented defective-rank family
$n=12s$ with $s$ squarefree and $(s,6)=1$.

The same lcm mechanism gives a local-to-global factorization over connected
coordinate blocks.

\begin{previewtheorem}[Connected block factorization]
Let $q\in B_n$ and form the support hypergraph on $\mathcal P(n)$ whose
edges are the prime-coordinate supports of the ranks of the prime-power
factors of $q$. If its connected components are $C$, then
\[
q=\prod_C q_C,\qquad q_C\in B_{n_C},
\qquad n_C=\prod_{\ell\in C}\ell^{\nu_\ell(n)} ,
\]
and this factorization is unique. The same statement holds for minimal
generators after replacing $B_{n_C}$ by $\mathcal M_{n_C}$. Consequently
$A(n)$ and $\#\mathcal M_n$ are partition sums over the connected counts.
This is proved in Section~\ref{sec:connected}.
\end{previewtheorem}

As secondary consequences, nonexceptional birth layers contain principal
intervals natural after choosing an exact-rank prime and Boolean subposets of
rank $\tau(n)-O(1)$; both are proved in the \suppref{section
``Supplementary witness-cover consequences''}. For odd $n$, every minimal
cover of $\mathcal P(n)$ is realized by
Corollary~\ref{cor:odd-rank-pure-universality}.

Section~\ref{sec:prelim} records the classical background;
Section~\ref{sec:upper-fibers} develops witness covers;
Section~\ref{sec:connected} proves both connected-block factorizations and the
exact total and connected support spectra; and
Section~\ref{sec:growth} proves rank-window deaggregation, fibotomic and
rank-pure bounds, and the entropy theorem before isolating the unresolved
arithmetic interface. Section~\ref{sec:questions} records its two boundary
questions. The \suppref{section ``Low-support classifications''} proves the
complete cases $\omega(n)\le3$; the supplementary sections on rank-pure
asymptotics and finite numerical evidence prove the theta/local-limit
consequences and document the exhaustive tables and case checks.

\begin{abstract}
For each positive integer $n$, let $B_n$ denote the set of divisors of the
Fibonacci number $F_n$ whose rank of apparition under the map $\alpha$ equals
$n$. We describe $B_n$ as an upper set in the divisor lattice of $F_n$ and
classify its minimal elements by irredundant witness covers of the prime
coordinates of $n$. The resulting support budget $\omega(m)\leq\omega(n)$ is
best possible as a uniform bound. We determine the complete total and
connected support spectra on every layer: the total spectrum misses its top
value exactly for even squarefree indices, while the connected spectrum has
one additional explicit rank-$6$ obstruction family. We construct a rank-pure exact-rank-prime
sector that embeds every irredundant cover of the prime coordinates except for
at most two explicitly identified exceptional supports, preserving the complete
support hypergraph. If $k=\omega(n)$ and $R(n)$ is the largest number of
atomic prime powers with one fixed essential/full support pair, then, uniformly
as $k\to\infty$,
\[
 \log\#\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\geq
 \frac{\log 2}{4}k^2+k\log2-\frac12\log k+O(1),
 \qquad
 \log\#\mathcal M_n
 \leq \frac{\log 2}{4}k^2+k\log R(n)+O(k).
\]
The lower bound has an explicit parity-dependent Jacobi theta constant and is
realized by connected minimal generators. It does not imply that disconnected
generators are negligible in the full arithmetic count. The upper estimate is
not an asymptotic formula without additional control of the unresolved quantity
$R(n)$. We prove that the mean contribution from aggregating several exact-rank
classes in one support window is only $O(x\log\log x)$, reducing the mean
interface to a visible single-rank maximum. We also show that a published
conjecture of Bugeaud--Luca--Mignotte--Siksek would contradict the resulting
mean sparse-window hypothesis, and give a blockwise condition under which
disconnected generators are exponentially negligible. Finally, we classify
the cases $\omega(n)\leq3$ and prove unique connected-block factorizations for
$B_n$ and its minimal elements.
For the remaining single-rank maximum, a fibotomic argument gives the
unconditional pointwise estimate
$a(d)\leq(\log\varphi/2+o(1))\phi_{\rm E}(d)/\log d$, where
$\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2$ and $\phi_{\rm E}$ is Euler's function, together with
new unconditional upper and lower bounds for its weighted mean contribution.
\end{abstract}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Keywords.} Fibonacci numbers, rank of apparition, divisor
lattices, upper sets, witness covers, atomic decompositions.

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{Mathematics Subject Classification (2020).}
11B39, 11A07, 11A25.

\section{Preliminaries}\label{sec:prelim}
%% ====================================================================

\subsection{Rank of apparition and Fibonacci divisibility}\label{subsec:fib-div}

\begin{lemma}[Finite rank of apparition]\label{lem:finite-rank}
For every integer $q\ge1$, the rank of apparition $\alpha(q)$ is finite.  More
precisely, $\alpha(1)=1$, and for every $q\ge2$ there is a positive integer
$r$ such that $q\mid F_r$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The case $q=1$ is the stated convention.  Let $q\ge2$.  Work modulo $q$ with
the Fibonacci state vector $(F_m,F_{m+1})$.  The transition
\[
(x,y)\longmapsto (y,x+y)
\]
has inverse $(x,y)\mapsto (y-x,x)$ modulo $q$, so it is a permutation of the
finite set $(\ZZ/q\ZZ)^2$.  Therefore the orbit of the initial state $(0,1)$
is periodic and returns to $(0,1)$ after some positive number $r$ of steps.
Thus $(F_r,F_{r+1})\equiv(0,1)\pmod q$, in particular $q\mid F_r$.  The
least such positive $r$ exists by well ordering.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:rank-basics}
Let $q,u,v\ge1$ and let $a,b,m\ge1$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $q\mid F_m$ if and only if $\alpha(q)\mid m$.
\item $\gcd(F_a,F_b)=F_{\gcd(a,b)}$.
\item $\alpha(\lcmop(u,v))=\lcmop(\alpha(u),\alpha(v))$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The divisibility criterion and the strong-divisibility identity are classical;
we use them in the forms proved by Wall \cite{Wall1960FibonacciModm} and
Vinson \cite{Vinson1963RankApparition}. The convention $\alpha(1)=1$ makes
the assertion for $q=1$ immediate.

For the lcm identity, an integer $m$ is divisible by
$\alpha(\lcmop(u,v))$ if and only if $\lcmop(u,v)\mid F_m$, by (i). This is
equivalent to $u\mid F_m$ and $v\mid F_m$, hence to
$\alpha(u)\mid m$ and $\alpha(v)\mid m$, again by (i). Thus the multiples of
$\alpha(\lcmop(u,v))$ are exactly the multiples of
$\lcmop(\alpha(u),\alpha(v))$, and the two positive integers are equal.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Exact-rank primes and primitive divisors}\label{subsec:primitive}

A prime $p$ is an \emph{exact-rank prime of rank $d$} if $\alpha(p)=d$;
when it is viewed as an atomic prime power, we also call it a \emph{prime
atom}. We reserve \emph{primitive divisor of $F_d$} for the standard
Carmichael--Bilu--Hanrot--Voutier convention: a prime $p$ dividing $F_d$ is
primitive when
\[
 p\nmid 5F_1F_2\cdots F_{d-1}.
\]
Thus every primitive divisor is an exact-rank prime, but the exact-rank prime
$5$ at rank $5$ is excluded from the primitive-divisor terminology because it
divides the discriminant. This distinction will be maintained throughout.

\begin{theorem}[Classical exact-rank existence]\label{thm:primitive-divisors}
For every $n\ge 3$ with $n\notin\{6,12\}$, there exists a prime $p_n$ with
$\alpha(p_n)=n$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $n=3,4,5$ one takes $p_3=2$, $p_4=3$, and $p_5=5$; the last is the
discriminant-related exact-rank exception just described. For $n\ge 7$,
$n\neq 12$, the primitive divisor theorem
\cite{Carmichael1913PrimitiveDivisors,Yabuta2001PrimitiveDivisorFibonacci,%
BiluHanrotVoutier2001PrimitiveDivisorsLucas}
gives a prime dividing $F_n$ but no earlier Fibonacci number. Since
$\alpha(p_n)\mid n$ and $p_n$ divides no $F_d$ with $d<n$, necessarily
$\alpha(p_n)=n$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Counting corollaries}\label{subsec:counting}

The standard M\"obius-inversion formulas for first-appearance counts follow
from Stroi\'nski's $\alpha$-contraction identity
\cite{Stroinski2016AlphaContraction}: for any $f:\NN\to\mathbb{C}$ with
$f(1)=0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:stroinski}
\sum_{d\mid F_n}f(d)=\sum_{d\mid n}f_\alpha(d),
\qquad
f_\alpha(d):=\sum_{\alpha(m)=d}f(m).
\end{equation}
Taking $f$ to be the indicator of integers at least $2$, of primes, or of
prime powers, and applying M\"obius inversion, one obtains
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mobius-birth}
A(n)=\sum_{d\mid n}\mu(n/d)\,\tau(F_d),
\qquad
A_{\mathrm{pr}}(n)=\sum_{d\mid n}\mu(n/d)\,\omega(F_d).
\end{equation}
We regard these as known corollaries; the results of
Sections~\ref{sec:upper-fibers}--\ref{sec:connected} are independent of them.

\begin{lemma}[Exact-rank prime M\"obius count]\label{lem:primitive-prime-mobius-count}
For $d\ge1$, put
\[
\Pi_\alpha(d):=\{p\text{ prime}:\alpha(p)=d\}.
\]
Then $\Pi_\alpha(d)$ is finite and
\[
\#\Pi_\alpha(d)=\sum_{c\mid d}\mu(d/c)\,\omega(F_c).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For each $d\ge1$, the primes with rank dividing $d$ are exactly the prime
divisors of $F_d$: indeed, for a prime $p$, Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics}
gives $p\mid F_d$ if and only if $\alpha(p)\mid d$.  Hence
Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics} partitions the distinct prime divisors of
$F_d$ according to their exact ranks $c\mid d$, including the exceptional
primes $2$ and $5$ with ranks $3$ and $5$ respectively; a prime divisor of
$F_c$ can occur earlier only through the condition $\alpha(p)\mid e$ for the
earlier index $e$.
\[
\omega(F_d)=\sum_{c\mid d}\#\Pi_\alpha(c).
\]
The right-hand side is finite because $F_d$ has only finitely many prime
divisors.  Applying M\"obius inversion on the divisor lattice of $d$ gives the
displayed formula.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Atomic notation}\label{subsec:prime-power}

\begin{definition}\label{def:atomic-families}
For a prime power $\theta=p^e$ with $e\ge1$, put
\[
d(\theta):=\alpha(\theta),\qquad
d^-(\theta):=\alpha(p^{e-1}),
\]
where $\alpha(1)=1$. For $d\ge1$ define
\[
\Atom(d):=\{p^e:\ \alpha(p^e)=d,\ \alpha(p^{e-1})<d\}.
\]
Thus $\Atom(d)$ consists of the prime-power factors that are born exactly at
rank $d$.

If $d(\theta)\mid n$, define the full-coordinate support and the
essential-coordinate support of $\theta$ relative to $n$ by
\[
T_n(\theta):=\{\ell\in\mathcal P(n):\
\nu_\ell(d(\theta))=\nu_\ell(n)\},
\]
and
\[
E_n(\theta):=\{\ell\in T_n(\theta):\
\nu_\ell(d^-(\theta))<\nu_\ell(n)\}.
\]
Equivalently, $T_n(\theta)$ records the prime coordinates of $n$ supplied at
full exponent by $d(\theta)$, while $E_n(\theta)$ records the full coordinates
lost when $\theta=p^e$ is lowered to $p^{e-1}$.

For nonempty $I\subseteq J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$, put
\[
\mathcal A_{I,J}(n):=
\{\theta\in\bigcup_{d\mid n}\Atom(d):\
E_n(\theta)=I,\ T_n(\theta)=J\}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:odd-primitive-cover-realization}
Let $n\ge3$ be odd. For each nonempty $S\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$ put
\[
n_S=\prod_{\ell\in S}\ell^{\nu_\ell(n)}.
\]
Choose an exact-rank prime $\pi_S$ with $\alpha(\pi_S)=n_S$, whose existence is
guaranteed by Theorem~\ref{thm:primitive-divisors}. Then every set partition
$\Pi$ of $\mathcal P(n)$ gives a minimal generator
\[
m_\Pi:=\prod_{C\in\Pi}\pi_C\in\mathcal M_n.
\]
Thus
\[
\#\mathcal M_n\ge \mathsf B_{\omega(n)},
\]
and, since the minimal generators form an antichain in the divisor lattice,
\[
\widthop(B_n)\ge \#\mathcal M_n\ge \mathsf B_{\omega(n)},
\]
where $\mathsf B_k$ is the $k$th Bell number.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Since $n$ is odd and $n\ge3$, every nonempty $n_S$ is odd and at least $3$;
therefore $n_S\notin\{6,12\}$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:primitive-divisors}
supplies the required exact-rank prime $\pi_S$. For each block $C$, the prime
$\pi_C$ is a prime atom of rank $n_C$ and
therefore belongs to $\mathcal M_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$: its support
hypergraph has the single edge $C$. Distinct blocks have coprime Fibonacci
ambient factors by Lemma~\ref{thm:coprime-embedding}, so the primes
$\pi_C$ are pairwise coprime. Theorem~\ref{thm:mingenerator-connected-factorization}
then gives $m_\Pi\in\mathcal M_n$.

The connected-component partition of $m_\Pi$ is exactly $\Pi$, so different
set partitions give different minimal generators. There are
$\mathsf B_{\omega(n)}$ set partitions of $\mathcal P(n)$.
Finally, no two distinct elements of $\mathcal M_n$ are comparable under
divisibility: if $m,m'\in\mathcal M_n$ and $m\mid m'$ with $m\ne m'$, then
$m'$ is not minimal in the upper set $B_n$. Hence $\mathcal M_n$ is an
antichain in $B_n$, so $\widthop(B_n)\ge\#\mathcal M_n$.
\end{proof}

\section{Connected block factorizations}\label{sec:connected}
%% ====================================================================

The hypergraph decomposition below is a generic consequence of strong
divisibility and disjoint coordinate supports. Its role here is structural:
the substantive endpoint for the present fibers is the induced factorization
of minimal elements and the associated partition formulas.

Throughout this section, write
$n_C:=\prod_{\ell\in C}\ell^{\nu_\ell(n)}$ for nonempty
$C\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$.

\begin{definition}\label{def:support-hypergraph}
Let $q=\prod_i p_i^{e_i}\in D_n$, and put
$\lambda_i:=\alpha(p_i^{e_i})$. The \emph{support hypergraph} $H_n(q)$ has
vertex set $\mathcal P(n)$ and one edge
\[
\operatorname{supp}_n(\lambda_i):=
\{\ell\in\mathcal P(n):\nu_\ell(\lambda_i)>0\}
\]
for each nonempty such support. We call $q$ \emph{connected} if
$H_n(q)$ is connected on its vertex set. Let
\[
B_n^{\mathrm{conn}}:=\{q\in B_n:\ H_n(q)\text{ is connected}\},\qquad
A^{\mathrm{conn}}(n):=\#B_n^{\mathrm{conn}},
\]
and
\[
\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}:=
\{m\in\mathcal M_n:\ H_n(m)\text{ is connected}\},\qquad
M^{\mathrm{conn}}(n):=\#\mathcal M_n^{\mathrm{conn}}.
\]
For a connected component $C$ of $H_n(q)$, define $q_C$ to be the product of
those prime-power factors $p_i^{e_i}$ whose support edge lies in $C$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Disjoint-block coprimality]\label{thm:coprime-embedding}
Let $\Pi$ be a set partition of $\mathcal P(n)$. For each block
$C\in\Pi$, choose $q_C\in D_{n_C}$. Then the integers $q_C$ are pairwise
coprime. If in addition $q_C\in B_{n_C}$ for every $C$, then
\[
q:=\prod_{C\in\Pi}q_C
\]
lies in $B_n$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If $C\ne C'$, then $\gcd(n_C,n_{C'})=1$, so
\[
\gcd(F_{n_C},F_{n_{C'}})=F_{\gcd(n_C,n_{C'})}=F_1=1
\]
by Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics}. Since $q_C\mid F_{n_C}$ and
$q_{C'}\mid F_{n_{C'}}$, the chosen factors are pairwise coprime.
Equivalently, any common prime divisor $p$ would satisfy
$\alpha(p)\mid n_C$ and $\alpha(p)\mid n_{C'}$, hence
$\alpha(p)\mid\gcd(n_C,n_{C'})=1$; this is impossible for a prime, and in
particular excludes the shared special prime $5$ and any shared exact-rank prime
across disjoint coordinate blocks.

When $q_C\in B_{n_C}$ for every $C$, we have $\alpha(q_C)=n_C$. Repeated use
of the lcm formula in Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics} gives
\[
\alpha\!\left(\prod_{C\in\Pi}q_C\right)
=\lcmop(n_C:\ C\in\Pi)=\prod_{C\in\Pi}n_C=n.
\]
Also $q_C\mid F_{n_C}\mid F_n$, so the pairwise coprime product divides
$F_n$. Hence $q\in B_n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:birth-connected-factorization}
Every $q\in B_n$ has a unique factorization
\[
q=\prod_{C\in\pi(q)}q_C
\]
indexed by the connected components $\pi(q)$ of $H_n(q)$, where
$q_C\in B_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$ for every component $C$. Conversely, if
$\Pi$ is a set partition of $\mathcal P(n)$ and
$q_C\in B_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$ for every $C\in\Pi$, then
$\prod_C q_C\in B_n$ and its connected components are exactly the blocks of
$\Pi$. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:partition-formula}
A(n)=\sum_{\Pi\in\mathsf{Part}(\mathcal P(n))}
\prod_{C\in\Pi}A^{\mathrm{conn}}(n_C),
\end{equation}
with the convention that an empty factor contributes zero whenever
$B_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}=\varnothing$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $q=\prod_i p_i^{e_i}\in B_n$ and let $C$ be a component of $H_n(q)$.
Every prime-power factor contributing to $q_C$ has rank support contained in
$C$, so $\alpha(q_C)$ divides $n_C$. Conversely, fix $\ell\in C$. Since
$\alpha(q)=n$ is the lcm of the ranks $\alpha(p_i^{e_i})$, some factor of
$q$ has $\ell$-adic rank exponent equal to $\nu_\ell(n)$. Its support edge
contains $\ell$, and therefore belongs to the component $C$. Thus the same
factor occurs in $q_C$, and the $\ell$-adic exponent of $\alpha(q_C)$ is
$\nu_\ell(n)$. This holds for every $\ell\in C$, while no coordinate outside
$C$ occurs in $\alpha(q_C)$; hence $\alpha(q_C)=n_C$ and
$q_C\in B_{n_C}$. The component of $H_{n_C}(q_C)$ on the vertex set $C$ is
connected by construction, so $q_C\in B_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$.

Uniqueness follows from unique prime-power factorization of $q$ and the
unique connected-component partition of a hypergraph. The converse is exactly
Lemma~\ref{thm:coprime-embedding}; because each $q_C$ is connected on its
own block and no edge of $H_n(\prod_C q_C)$ meets two different blocks, the
components are precisely the blocks of $\Pi$.

Counting the converse construction over all set partitions gives
\eqref{eq:partition-formula}, and uniqueness shows that no element is counted
twice.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:mingenerator-connected-factorization}
Every $m\in\mathcal M_n$ has a unique factorization
\[
m=\prod_{C\in\pi(m)}m_C
\]
over the connected components of $H_n(m)$, with
$m_C\in\mathcal M_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$ for every $C$. Conversely, if
$\Pi$ is a set partition of $\mathcal P(n)$ and
$m_C\in\mathcal M_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$ for every $C\in\Pi$, then
$\prod_C m_C\in\mathcal M_n$ and has component partition $\Pi$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:minimal-partition-formula}
\#\mathcal M_n=
\sum_{\Pi\in\mathsf{Part}(\mathcal P(n))}
\prod_{C\in\Pi}M^{\mathrm{conn}}(n_C).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $m\in\mathcal M_n$. Theorem~\ref{thm:birth-connected-factorization}
gives $m_C\in B_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$ for each component. If some
$s_C\in B_{n_C}$ satisfied $s_C\mid m_C$ and $s_C<m_C$, then
\[
s_C\prod_{D\ne C}m_D
\]
would divide $m$. The factors are pairwise coprime by
Lemma~\ref{thm:coprime-embedding}, and the displayed product lies in
$B_n$ by the same theorem. This contradicts the minimality of $m$ in $B_n$.
Therefore $m_C\in\mathcal M_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$.

Conversely, let $m=\prod_{C\in\Pi}m_C$ with
$m_C\in\mathcal M_{n_C}^{\mathrm{conn}}$. Lemma~\ref{thm:coprime-embedding}
puts $m$ in $B_n$. If $s\mid m$ and $s\in B_n$, write
$s=\prod_C s_C$ with $s_C\mid m_C$; pairwise coprimeness of the block
factors makes this decomposition unique. Since
\[
n=\alpha(s)=\lcmop(\alpha(s_C):C\in\Pi)
\]
and $\alpha(s_C)$ divides $n_C$, the disjointness of the coordinate sets
forces $\alpha(s_C)=n_C$ for every $C$. Thus $s_C\in B_{n_C}$ for every
$C$, and minimality of $m_C$ gives $s_C=m_C$. Hence $s=m$, proving
$m\in\mathcal M_n$. The component statement and uniqueness are the same as
in Theorem~\ref{thm:birth-connected-factorization}. Counting unique
decompositions yields \eqref{eq:minimal-partition-formula}.
\end{proof}

\section{Birth layers and witness covers}\label{sec:upper-fibers}
%% ====================================================================

The first two lemmas in this section use only rank divisibility and the lcm
identity. They are included to fix the structural framework and are not
presented as new Fibonacci identities. The witness-cover theorem packages
their minimality conditions for the subsequent Fibonacci analysis.

\begin{definition}\label{def:birth-layer}
For $n\ge2$ let
\[
D_n:=\operatorname{Div}(F_n),\qquad
B_n:=\{q\in D_n:\ \alpha(q)=n\},\qquad
A(n):=\#B_n.
\]
The set $B_n$ is partially ordered by divisibility. Its set of minimal
elements is
\[
\mathcal M_n:=\{m\in B_n:\ \text{there is no }q\in B_n
\text{ with }q\mid m\text{ and }q<m\},
\]
and
\[
\widthop(B_n):=\max\{\#\mathcal A:\ \mathcal A\subseteq B_n
\text{ is an antichain}\}.
\]
We write $\mathcal P(n)$ for the set of prime divisors of $n$. Since
$F_2=1$, we use the boundary convention
\[
B_2=\mathcal M_2=\varnothing,\qquad A(2)=\widthop(B_2)=0.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Upper-fiber identity]\label{thm:upper-fiber}
For every $n\ge2$,
\[
B_n=D_n\setminus\bigcup_{p\mid n}D_{n/p}.
\]
Moreover $B_n$ is an upper set in $D_n$: if $q\in B_n$, $r\in D_n$, and
$q\mid r$, then $r\in B_n$. Consequently
\[
B_n=\bigcup_{m\in\mathcal M_n}\{q\in D_n:\ m\mid q\}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics}, an integer $q$ lies in $D_m$ if and
only if $\alpha(q)\mid m$. Thus $q\in D_n$ lies outside
$\bigcup_{p\mid n}D_{n/p}$ precisely when $\alpha(q)\mid n$ but
$\alpha(q)\nmid n/p$ for every prime $p\mid n$.

If $\alpha(q)=n$, then clearly $\alpha(q)\nmid n/p$ for every $p\mid n$, so
$q$ is outside the union. Conversely, suppose $\alpha(q)\mid n$ and
$\alpha(q)<n$. Then $n/\alpha(q)>1$; choose a prime $p$ dividing
$n/\alpha(q)$. Since $\alpha(q)\mid n/p$, Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics}
gives $q\in D_{n/p}$, contrary to being outside the union. Hence the
complement is exactly the set of $q\in D_n$ with $\alpha(q)=n$, namely
$B_n$. For $n=2$ this also gives
$B_2=D_2\setminus D_1=\varnothing$ under the boundary convention in
Definition~\ref{def:birth-layer}.

Now let $q\in B_n$, $r\in D_n$, and $q\mid r$. Since $r\in D_n$,
$\alpha(r)\mid n$. Since $q\mid r$, every Fibonacci number divisible by $r$
is divisible by $q$; applying the divisibility criterion to
$F_{\alpha(r)}$ gives $\alpha(q)\mid\alpha(r)$. But $\alpha(q)=n$, so
$n\mid\alpha(r)$ and $\alpha(r)\mid n$, whence $\alpha(r)=n$. Therefore
$r\in B_n$.

Finally $D_n$ is finite, so every element $q\in B_n$ has at least one
minimal element $m$ of $B_n$ below it in the divisibility order. The upper-set
property gives the reverse containment: every element of $D_n$ divisible by
some $m\in\mathcal M_n$ lies again in $B_n$. This proves the displayed
decomposition.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Minimal-lcm criterion]\label{thm:minimal-generators}
Let $n\ge2$, and let
$m=\prod_{i=1}^r p_i^{e_i}$ be a divisor of $F_n$, with the $p_i$ distinct
and $e_i\ge1$. Put
\[
\lambda_i:=\alpha(p_i^{e_i}),\qquad
\lambda_i^-:=\alpha(p_i^{e_i-1}),
\]
where $\alpha(1)=1$. Then $m\in\mathcal M_n$ if and only if
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lcm-criterion}
\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r)=n
\quad\text{and}\quad
\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{i-1},\lambda_i^-,
\lambda_{i+1},\ldots,\lambda_r)<n
\quad(1\le i\le r).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Because $m\mid F_n$, Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics} gives
$\lambda_i\mid n$ for every $i$. Applying the lcm identity in
Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics} repeatedly gives
\[
\alpha(m)=\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r).
\]
Thus the first condition in \eqref{eq:lcm-criterion} is equivalent to
$m\in B_n$.

Assume first that $m\in\mathcal M_n$. Then $m\in B_n$, so the first lcm is
$n$. For a fixed $i$, the integer $m/p_i$ is a proper divisor of $m$ and is
still a divisor of $F_n$. Minimality of $m$ in $B_n$ implies
$m/p_i\notin B_n$. Since $\alpha(m/p_i)\mid n$, this is equivalent to
$\alpha(m/p_i)<n$. A second application of the lcm identity gives
\[
\alpha(m/p_i)=
\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{i-1},\lambda_i^-,
\lambda_{i+1},\ldots,\lambda_r),
\]
which is the lowered prime-power condition in
\eqref{eq:lcm-criterion}.

Conversely, suppose \eqref{eq:lcm-criterion} holds. The first condition gives
$m\in B_n$. Let $q$ be any proper divisor of $m$. Then for at least one
index $i$ the exponent of $p_i$ in $q$ is at most $e_i-1$. Hence
\[
\alpha(q)\mid
\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{i-1},\lambda_i^-,
\lambda_{i+1},\ldots,\lambda_r),
\]
again by the lcm identity and by monotonicity under divisibility of prime
powers. The right-hand side is $<n$ by
\eqref{eq:lcm-criterion}. Since $q\mid m\mid F_n$, we also have
$\alpha(q)\mid n$, and therefore $\alpha(q)<n$. Thus no proper divisor of
$m$ lies in $B_n$, so $m$ is minimal in $B_n$.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{Witness covers}

\begin{definition}\label{def:witness-cover}
An \emph{$n$-witness cover} is a finite set
$\Theta=\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_r\}$ of atomic prime powers satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $d(\theta)\mid n$ for every $\theta\in\Theta$;
\item the elements of $\Theta$ are pairwise coprime;
\item the full-coordinate supports cover all prime coordinates of $n$,
\[
\bigcup_{\theta\in\Theta}T_n(\theta)=\mathcal P(n);
\]
\item each atom has an essential coordinate not supplied by the other atoms,
\[
E_n(\theta)\nsubseteq
\bigcup_{\eta\in\Theta,\ \eta\ne\theta}T_n(\eta)
\qquad(\theta\in\Theta).
\]
\end{enumerate}
The last condition is the irredundance condition. It is stated with
$E_n$ rather than $T_n$ because overlapping full-coordinate support is
allowed; what is forbidden is that every coordinate lost by lowering
$\theta$ is already supplied at full exponent by the remaining atoms.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Coordinate test for lowering one atom]
\label{lem:coordinate-lowering-test}
Let $n\ge2$, and let
$\Theta=\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_r\}$ be a pairwise coprime set of atomic
prime powers with $d(\theta_i)\mid n$ and
\[
\lcmop(d(\theta_1),\ldots,d(\theta_r))=n.
\]
Fix $\theta_i=p_i^{e_i}$, and put
\[
N_i^-:=\lcmop\bigl(d^-(\theta_i),\ d(\theta_j):j\ne i\bigr).
\]
Then $N_i^-=n$ if and only if
\[
E_n(\theta_i)\subseteq\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j).
\]
Equivalently, lowering $\theta_i$ to $p_i^{e_i-1}$ keeps the apparition lcm
equal to $n$ exactly when every coordinate lost by the lowering is supplied at
full exponent by some other atom. If
$\ell\in E_n(\theta_i)\setminus\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j)$, then
$\nu_\ell(N_i^-)<\nu_\ell(n)$, so $N_i^-<n$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since $d^-(\theta_i)\mid d(\theta_i)\mid n$ and $d(\theta_j)\mid n$ for
$j\ne i$, the integer $N_i^-$ divides $n$. Hence $N_i^-=n$ is equivalent to
saying that every prime coordinate $\ell\in\mathcal P(n)$ occurs in $N_i^-$
with valuation $\nu_\ell(n)$.

Assume first that
$E_n(\theta_i)\subseteq\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j)$, and fix
$\ell\in\mathcal P(n)$. Because the original lcm is $n$, at least one of the
numbers $d(\theta_1),\ldots,d(\theta_r)$ supplies the full
$\ell$-adic valuation of $n$. If such a supplier is some $d(\theta_j)$ with
$j\ne i$, then $\ell\in T_n(\theta_j)$ and the same full valuation appears in
$N_i^-$. The only remaining case is that $\theta_i$ itself supplies the full
valuation and no other atom does, so $\ell\in T_n(\theta_i)$ and
$\ell\notin\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j)$. The assumed containment then gives
$\ell\notin E_n(\theta_i)$. By the definition of $E_n(\theta_i)$,
$\nu_\ell(d^-(\theta_i))=\nu_\ell(n)$, so the lowered factor still supplies the
full $\ell$-coordinate in $N_i^-$. Thus every coordinate of $n$ is still
present at full valuation, and $N_i^-=n$.

Conversely, suppose that some coordinate
$\ell\in E_n(\theta_i)$ is not in $\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j)$. The
membership $\ell\in E_n(\theta_i)$ says
$\nu_\ell(d^-(\theta_i))<\nu_\ell(n)$. For every $j\ne i$, the exclusion
$\ell\notin T_n(\theta_j)$ says
$\nu_\ell(d(\theta_j))<\nu_\ell(n)$. Therefore the lcm defining $N_i^-$ also
has $\ell$-adic valuation strictly below $\nu_\ell(n)$. Since $N_i^-\mid n$,
this proves $N_i^-<n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:witness-classification}
For every $n\ge 2$, multiplication gives a bijection
\[
\Theta\longmapsto \prod_{\theta\in\Theta}\theta
\]
from the set of $n$-witness covers onto $\mathcal M_n$. Its inverse sends
$m\in\mathcal M_n$ to the set
\[
\Theta(m):=\{p^{\nu_p(m)}:p\mid m\}
\]
of prime-power factors occurring in the prime factorization of $m$. In
particular,
\[
\mathcal M_n=\bigl\{\prod_{\theta\in\Theta}\theta:
\Theta\text{ is an $n$-witness cover}\bigr\},
\]
and each element of $\mathcal M_n$ has a unique witness-cover factorization.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $m\in\mathcal M_n$, and write its prime factorization as
$m=\prod_{i=1}^r\theta_i$ with $\theta_i=p_i^{e_i}$ and the $p_i$ distinct.
Each $\theta_i$ is atomic: if $\alpha(p_i^{e_i-1})=\alpha(p_i^{e_i})$, then
replacing $\theta_i$ by $p_i^{e_i-1}$ preserves the lcm, contradicting
the minimal-generator criterion in
Lemma~\ref{thm:minimal-generators}. Since
$n=\lcmop(d(\theta_i))$, the sets $T_n(\theta_i)$ cover $\mathcal P(n)$. The
condition $\alpha(m/p_i)<n$ forces
$E_n(\theta_i)\nsubseteq\bigcup_{j\neq i}T_n(\theta_j)$; otherwise the lost
coordinates would be supplied by other factors, keeping the lcm equal to $n$.
Thus $\{\theta_1,\dots,\theta_r\}$ is an $n$-witness cover.

Conversely, let $\Theta$ be an $n$-witness cover and
$m=\prod_{\theta\in\Theta}\theta$. By
the lcm property recorded in Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics},
$\alpha(m)=\lcmop(d(\theta):\theta\in\Theta)=n$. For each
$\theta=p^e\in\Theta$, the witness condition
provides $\ell\in E_n(\theta)\setminus\bigcup_{\eta\neq\theta}T_n(\eta)$; at
this coordinate, $\alpha(m/p)<n$.
Lemma~\ref{thm:minimal-generators} then gives $m\in\mathcal M_n$.

It remains to record injectivity and the stated inverse.  If two witness covers
$\Theta$ and $\Theta'$ have the same product, then unique factorization in
$\mathbb Z$ gives the same underlying primes with the same exponents in the two
products.  Since the elements of each witness cover are pairwise coprime prime
powers, those prime-power factors are exactly the elements of the cover.  Hence
$\Theta=\Theta'$.  Therefore multiplication is injective, while the first
paragraph shows that the prime-power factor set $\Theta(m)$ of every
$m\in\mathcal M_n$ is an $n$-witness cover and the second paragraph shows that
every $n$-witness cover maps into $\mathcal M_n$.  Thus multiplication is the
claimed bijection, with inverse $m\mapsto\Theta(m)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:support-bound}
Every $m\in\mathcal M_n$ satisfies $\omega(m)\le\omega(n)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The witness condition provides an injection
$\theta\mapsto\ell(\theta)$ from $\Theta$ into $\mathcal P(n)$: choose
$\ell(\theta)\in E_n(\theta)\setminus\bigcup_{\eta\neq\theta}T_n(\eta)$.
Hence $\omega(m)=\#\Theta\le\omega(n)$.
\end{proof}



[32m succeeded in 173ms:[0m
\begin{theorem}[Exact support spectra and the extremal slice]
\label{thm:exact-support-spectra}
Let $n\ge3$, write
\[
 n=\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal P(n)}\ell^{a_\ell},
 \qquad k=\omega(n),
\]
and put
\[
 \Sigma(n):=\{\omega(m):m\in\mathcal M_n\},\qquad
 \Sigma^{\rm conn}(n):=\{\omega(m):m\in\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\}.
\]
For each $\ell\in\mathcal P(n)$, define the singleton diagonal atomic family
\[
 \mathcal A_\ell(n):=
 \mathcal A_{\{\ell\},\{\ell\}}(n).
\]
Then multiplication gives a bijection
\begin{equation}\label{eq:extremal-support-product-bijection}
 \prod_{\ell\in\mathcal P(n)}\mathcal A_\ell(n)
 \longrightarrow
 \{m\in\mathcal M_n:\omega(m)=k\},
 \qquad (\theta_\ell)_\ell\longmapsto
 \prod_{\ell\in\mathcal P(n)}\theta_\ell.
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:extremal-support-product-count}
 \#\{m\in\mathcal M_n:\omega(m)=k\}
 =\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal P(n)}\#\mathcal A_\ell(n).
\end{equation}
The complete support spectrum is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-total-support-spectrum}
 \Sigma(n)=
 \begin{cases}
  \{1,\ldots,k-1\},&n\text{ is even and squarefree},\\
  \{1,\ldots,k\},&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
For the connected spectrum, $\Sigma^{\rm conn}(n)=\{1\}$ when $k=1$.
If $k\ge2$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-connected-support-spectrum}
 \Sigma^{\rm conn}(n)=
 \begin{cases}
  \{1,\ldots,k-1\},
   &\begin{array}{l}
     n\text{ is squarefree, or }n=12s\text{ with }s\text{ squarefree}\
     \text{and }\gcd(s,6)=1,
    \end{array}\\[2mm]
  \{1,\ldots,k\},&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We first classify the extremal slice. Let $m\in\mathcal M_n$ have
$\omega(m)=k$, and let $\Theta(m)$ be its witness cover. For every
$\theta\in\Theta(m)$, choose a private coordinate
\[
 \ell(\theta)\in E_n(\theta)\setminus
 \bigcup_{\eta\in\Theta(m),\ \eta\ne\theta}T_n(\eta).
\]
The chosen coordinates are distinct, since a coordinate chosen for two atoms
would lie in the full support of each and hence would not be private to either.
There are $k$ atoms and $k$ coordinates, so $\theta\mapsto\ell(\theta)$ is a
bijection. If $q\ne\ell(\theta)$, then $q$ is private to another atom and
therefore cannot lie in $T_n(\theta)$. Hence
\[
 T_n(\theta)=E_n(\theta)=\{\ell(\theta)\}.
\]
Thus the inverse of \eqref{eq:extremal-support-product-bijection} sends $m$
to its uniquely labelled atoms in the families $\mathcal A_\ell(n)$.

Conversely, choose $\theta_\ell\in\mathcal A_\ell(n)$ for every
$\ell\in\mathcal P(n)$.
These atoms are pairwise coprime. Indeed, if
$\theta_\ell=p^e$ and $\theta_q=p^f$ had the same underlying prime, then
$e\ne f$ because the same atom cannot lie in two differently labelled
families. Say $e<f$. Then $p^e\mid p^{f-1}$ would give
\[
 d(\theta_\ell)\mid d^-(\theta_q).
\]
Since $\ell$ occurs to its full $n$-adic exponent in $d(\theta_\ell)$, it
would also occur fully in $d(\theta_q)$, contrary to
$T_n(\theta_q)=\{q\}$ and $q\ne\ell$. The singleton full supports cover
$\mathcal P(n)$, and the singleton essential supports are private. Hence the
chosen atoms form an $n$-witness cover. The witness-cover theorem proves that
their product belongs to $\mathcal M_n$ and has $k$ distinct prime factors.
Uniqueness follows from unique factorization. This proves the bijection and
\eqref{eq:extremal-support-product-count}.

We next decide when the factors on the right of
\eqref{eq:extremal-support-product-count} are nonzero. If $\ell$ is odd, or
if $\ell=2$ and $a_2\ge2$, then the exact-rank existence theorem supplies a
prime of rank $\ell^{a_\ell}$; this is an element of
$\mathcal A_\ell(n)$. Suppose now that $a_2=1$. If $n$ is squarefree and
$\theta\in\mathcal A_2(n)$, then $d(\theta)\mid n$ cannot contain an odd
coordinate: every such coordinate would occur fully and would belong to
$T_n(\theta)$. Thus $d(\theta)=2$, which is impossible because $F_2=1$ and
$\Atom(2)=\varnothing$. Therefore the extremal slice is empty when $n$ is
even and squarefree.

If instead $a_2=1$ and $q^2\mid n$ for some odd prime $q$, take an atom of
rank $2q$. For $q=3$, the atom $4\in\Atom(6)$ has, relative to $n$,
$E_n(4)=T_n(4)=\{2\}$. For $q\ge5$, the rank $2q$ is nonexceptional, so an
exact-rank prime exists and again has essential and full support $\{2\}$,
because the $q$-coordinate is sub-full in $n$. Thus
$\mathcal A_2(n)\ne\varnothing$. All other diagonal families were already
seen to be nonempty, proving that the support value $k$ occurs exactly in the
second case of \eqref{eq:exact-total-support-spectrum}.

It remains to realize every smaller support size. Assume $k\ge2$ and
$1\le r\le k-1$. Choose a partition $\Pi$ of $\mathcal P(n)$ into $r$
nonempty blocks, with the additional condition that $\{2\}$ is not a block
when $a_2=1$. Such a partition exists because $r<k$. For $C\in\Pi$, put
$n_C=\prod_{\ell\in C}\ell^{a_\ell}$ and choose
$\theta_C\in\Atom(n_C)$. This is possible by exact-rank prime existence
unless $n_C\in\{6,12\}$; in those two cases use respectively
$4\in\Atom(6)$ and $16\in\Atom(12)$. The forbidden rank $2$ was excluded by
the choice of $\Pi$. Strong divisibility makes the atoms belonging to
disjoint blocks pairwise coprime. Their full supports are the blocks $C$, and
each has a nonempty essential support because lowering it decreases its rank.
They therefore form an $n$-witness cover. Its product has exactly $r$ prime
factors, completing the proof of \eqref{eq:exact-total-support-spectrum}.

The same idea gives all connected support sizes below $k$. Choose a hub
$h\in\mathcal P(n)$, taking $h=3$ whenever
$\{2,3\}\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$ and $n_{\{2,3\}}\in\{6,12\}$, and partition
$\mathcal P(n)\setminus\{h\}$ into $r$ nonempty sets $P_1,\ldots,P_r$.
Use one atom of exact rank $n_{\{h\}\cup P_i}$ for each $i$. The only
exceptional support that can occur is $\{2,3\}$; use $4$ at rank $6$ or $16$
at rank $12$. In both cases its essential support contains the private
coordinate $2\in P_i$. Every nonexceptional atom is a prime atom and has any
coordinate of the nonempty set $P_i$ as a private coordinate. The atoms are
pairwise coprime: their ranks are distinct, and the exceptional atom has
underlying prime $2$, whose rank $3$ is not one of the other multi-coordinate
ranks. The resulting witness cover is connected because all its rank-support
edges contain $h$. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:connected-spectrum-lower-interval}
 \{1,\ldots,k-1\}\subseteq\Sigma^{\rm conn}(n)\qquad(k\ge2).
\end{equation}

We finally decide whether the endpoint $k$ can be connected. In an extremal
witness cover, write $\theta_\ell$ for the atom with
$T_n(\theta_\ell)=\{\ell\}$ and let
$S_\ell=\operatorname{supp}_n(d(\theta_\ell))$ be its positive-coordinate
rank support. If $q\in S_\ell\setminus\{\ell\}$, then
\[
 1\le\nu_q(d(\theta_\ell))<a_q,
\]
so necessarily $a_q\ge2$. If $n$ is squarefree, every $S_\ell$ is therefore
the singleton $\{\ell\}$, and the rank-support hypergraph is disconnected
when $k\ge2$.

There is one further obstruction. Let $n=12s$ with $s$ squarefree and
$\gcd(s,6)=1$. The only coordinate of exponent at least two is $2$. Hence an
edge $S_3$ larger than $\{3\}$ would force $d(\theta_3)=6$. But
$\Atom(6)=\{4\}$, and lowering $4$ gives rank $3$; relative to this $n$, the
$3$-coordinate is not lost. Thus an atom of rank $6$ with
$T_n(\theta_3)=\{3\}$ has empty essential support and cannot occur in a
witness cover. Consequently $S_3=\{3\}$. Since $a_3=1$, the coordinate $3$
cannot occur sub-fully in any other $S_\ell$, so it is isolated and the
extremal generator is disconnected.

Conversely, suppose that $n$ is nonsquarefree and is not of the preceding
form. Choose a coordinate $h$ with $a_h\ge2$. If possible take $h\ne2$. For
each $\ell\ne h$, choose an atom of rank
\[
 d_\ell=\ell^{a_\ell}h,
\]
and choose an exact-rank prime of rank $h^{a_h}$ for the $h$-atom. The ranks
$d_\ell$ are nonexceptional except possibly when $h=3$ and $\ell=2$; use
$4$ when $d_2=6$ and $16$ when $d_2=12$. These exceptional atoms have
$E_n=T_n=\{2\}$ because the $3$-coordinate is sub-full. If the only possible
hub is $h=2$, then either $3\nmid n$ or $a_2\ge3$. In the latter case, use
$9\in\Atom(12)$ for the $3$-atom; relative to $n$ it has
$E_n(9)=T_n(9)=\{3\}$. Use exact-rank primes of rank
$\ell^{a_\ell}2$ for the remaining nonhub coordinates. In every case we have
chosen one atom from each $\mathcal A_\ell(n)$, so the extremal-slice
bijection makes their product a minimal generator. Its rank-support edges are
$\{h\}$ and the pairs $\{h,\ell\}$, hence it is connected. Together with
\eqref{eq:connected-spectrum-lower-interval}, this proves
\eqref{eq:exact-connected-support-spectrum}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:support-bound-sharp}
For $n\ge3$, there exists $m\in\mathcal M_n$ with
$\omega(m)=\omega(n)$ if and only if $n$ is not even and squarefree. In
particular, equality occurs on every odd layer.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
This is the endpoint assertion in \eqref{eq:exact-total-support-spectrum}.
\end{proof}
